<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> We prove Toda's normalized quintic Gepner conjecture. On every smooth complex quintic threefold, we construct a numerical Bridgeland stability condition for which tensoring by the hyperplane bundle, followed by the spherical twist at the structure sheaf, increases phase by 2/5.

- **claim-main-gepner-stability** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-main)): Every smooth complex quintic threefold admits a numerical Bridgeland stability condition $\sigma=(Z,\mathcal P)$ with the support property on $K_{\mathrm{num}}(X)_{\mathbb R}$ such that, for every $E\in\mathcal D$ and $\varphi\in\mathbb R$, $$Z(\Phi E)=e^{2\pi i/5}Z(E),\qquad \Phi\bigl(\mathcal P(\varphi)\bigr)=\mathcal P(\varphi+2/5),\qquad Z(\mathcal O_x)=-1$$ for every closed point $x\in X$.
  - Explanation: Every smooth quintic threefold carries a numerical Bridgeland stability condition with the support property on the full numerical space, in which the autoequivalence rotates the charge and shifts phases by a fixed amount, and point sheaves have the normalized charge.
- **claim-periodicity** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-periodicity)): Let $\Phi=T_{\mathcal O_X}\circ(-\otimes\mathcal O_X(1))$. There is a natural isomorphism of exact functors $$\label{eq:periodicity} \Phi^5\simeq[2].$$ Consequently $\Phi$ is an autoequivalence, with inverse $\Phi^4[-2]$.
  - Explanation: The fifth iterate of the twisted autoequivalence is a double shift, so the functor is an autoequivalence with an explicit inverse.
- **claim-numerical-lattice** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-numerical)): The map $v$ identifies $K_{\mathrm{num}}(X)_{\mathbb R}$ with $\mathbb R^4$. Under this identification, $K_{\mathrm{num}}(X)$ is a full discrete lattice contained in $$\label{eq:numerical-lattice} \mathbb Z\times\mathbb Z\times\tfrac1{10}\mathbb Z\times\tfrac1{30}\mathbb Z.$$ For $v=(r,c,d,e)$ and $w=(r',c',d',e')$, the Euler form is $$\label{eq:euler-form} \chi(v,w)=5\left(re'-cd'+dc'-er' +\frac56(rc'-cr')\right).$$ The matrix induced by $\Phi$ is $$\label{eq:numerical-rotation} M= \begin{pmatrix} -4&-20/3&-5&-5\\ 1&1&0&0\\ 1/2&1&1&0\\ 1/6&1/2&1&1 \end{pmatrix}, \qquad \det(zI-M)=z^4+z^3+z^2+z+1.$$
  - Explanation: The real numerical group is four-dimensional in the normalized coordinates, the integral group is a full discrete lattice, and the Euler form and the matrix of the autoequivalence are given explicitly with an explicit characteristic polynomial.
- **claim-central-charge-unique** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-central-charge)): There is a unique numerical homomorphism $Z\colon K_{\mathrm{num}}(X)\to\mathbb C$ such that $Z\circ\Phi=\lambda Z$ and $Z(\mathcal O_x)=-1$ for closed points $x\in X$. It is $$\label{eq:central-charge} \frac{Z(r,c,d,e)}5 =\frac{\lambda-1}{5}r +\left(t_0^2-\frac56+\frac{i t_0}{2}\right)c +\left(-\frac12+i t_0\right)d-e.$$ In particular, $$\label{eq:imaginary-charge} \frac{\operatorname{Im}Z(r,c,d,e)}{5t_0}=d+\frac12c+ur.$$ For any fixed norm on $K_{\mathrm{num}}(X)_{\mathbb R}$, there is a constant $C_0>0$ such that every real numerical class $v=(r,c,d,e)$ satisfies $$\label{eq:numerical-norm-control} \left\lVert v\right\rVert\le C_0\bigl(\|r\|+\|c\|+\|Z(v)\|\bigr).$$
  - Explanation: The normalized eigencharge exists and is unique; it has an explicit formula, its imaginary part is an explicit linear form, and rank, first Chern coordinate and charge control the whole numerical class.
- **bound-slope-envelope** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-slope-input)): Put $$f(x)=\max\left\{-\frac{3x}{25},\ \frac{x}{2}-\frac7{25},\ \frac{28x}{25}-\frac{31}{50}\right\} \qquad(0\leq x\leq1).$$ Every torsion-free slope-semistable sheaf $F$ on $X$ with $\|\mu(F)\|\leq1$ satisfies $$\label{eq:slope-envelope} \frac{d(F)}{r(F)}\leq f(\|\mu(F)\|).$$ The estimate extends under integral twists by $(x,y)\mapsto(x+n,y+nx+n^2/2)$. In particular, $f(x)\leq0$ for $0\leq x\leq1/2$, and $f(1/2)=-3/100$.
  - Explanation: A piecewise linear envelope bounds the normalized second character over rank of slope-semistable sheaves; it follows from an external stronger Bogomolov–Gieseker inequality and is stable under integral twists.
- **claim-first-tilt-noetherian** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-first-tilt-noetherian)): The abelian categories $\mathcal B_{-1/2}$ and $\mathcal B_{1/2}$ are noetherian.
  - Explanation: The two first tilted hearts satisfy the ascending chain condition on quotients, which is what the second tilt needs to build maximal torsion subobjects.
- **claim-second-tilt-torsion-pair** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-second-tilt)): For every $b=\pm1/2$ and $\eta\in B_\delta$, the pair $(\mathcal T_b^\eta,\mathcal F_b^\eta)$ is a torsion pair. Its tilt has bounded heart and aisle $$\mathcal A_b^\eta=\langle\mathcal F_b^\eta[1],\mathcal T_b^\eta\rangle, \qquad \mathcal U_b^\eta =\{E\in W_b:H^0_{\mathcal B_b}(E)\in\mathcal T_b^\eta\}.$$ On $\mathcal A_b^\eta$ one has $N_b^\eta\geq0$, and no nonzero object of this heart has zero numerical class.
  - Explanation: For every perturbation parameter in the small square the second cut gives a torsion pair in each first tilted heart; the resulting heart has a nonnegative form and contains no nonzero object of zero class.
- **claim-compare-slope-cuts** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-compare-slope-cuts)): For every $\alpha,\theta\in B_\delta$, $$\mathcal U_+^\alpha\subset\mathcal U_-^\theta.$$
  - Explanation: The aisle built at the positive first slope sits inside the aisle built at the negative first slope for all independent parameter choices.
- **claim-twist-aisle** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-twist-aisle)): For all $\alpha,\theta\in B_\delta$, $$\mathcal O_X\in\mathcal A_-^\theta,\qquad \mathcal O_X[2]\in\mathcal A_+^\alpha,\qquad T_{\mathcal O_X}(\mathcal U_+^\alpha)\subset\mathcal U_-^\theta.$$
  - Explanation: The structure sheaf and its double shift lie in the appropriate hearts, and the spherical twist carries the positive-slope aisle into the negative-slope aisle.
- **claim-uniform-aisle** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-uniform-aisle)): There is a fixed open square $$\label{eq:fixed-omega} \Omega=\{\eta=(\eta_0,\eta_1):\|\eta_0\|,\|\eta_1\|<5\cdot10^{-6}\}$$ and bounded aisles $U_\eta=\mathcal U_-^\eta$, with hearts $\mathcal A_\eta=U_\eta\cap(U_\eta[1])^\perp$, such that $$\label{eq:uniform-aisle} \Phi U_\eta\subset U_\theta \qquad(\eta,\theta\in\Omega).$$ Moreover $$\label{eq:perturbed-positive-form} N_\eta=d+\frac12c+ur+\eta_1c+\eta_0r\geq0 \quad\text{on }\mathcal A_\eta, \qquad N_0=\frac{\operatorname{Im}Z}{5t_0},$$ and no nonzero object of any $\mathcal A_\eta$ has zero numerical class. The two parameters in <a href="#eq:uniform-aisle" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:uniform-aisle">[eq:uniform-aisle]</a> vary independently throughout this same square.
  - Explanation: On one fixed open square of parameters the autoequivalence maps each aisle into every other aisle of the family, with independent parameters, and the perturbed form is nonnegative on the hearts.
- **claim-fine-grid** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-fine-grid)): There is an open square $\Omega_1$ about zero, contained in $\Omega$, such that $$\label{eq:single-shift-inclusion} D_3^\alpha\subset D_0^\beta[1] \qquad(\alpha,\beta\in\Omega_1).$$ For $k\in\mathbb Z$ and $\eta\in\Omega_1$, define $$\label{eq:fifths-aisles} V_k^\eta=\Phi^mU_\eta[j],\qquad k=2m+5j.$$ This is independent of the integers $m,j$ chosen, and these bounded aisles satisfy $$\label{eq:fifths-nesting} \begin{gathered} V_{k+1}^\alpha\subset V_k^\beta,\qquad V_{k+5}^\eta=V_k^\eta[1],\qquad \Phi V_k^\eta=V_{k+2}^\eta,\\ k\in\mathbb Z,\quad \alpha,\beta,\eta\in\Omega_1. \end{gathered}$$
  - Explanation: Shrinking the square gives an extra inclusion that refines the coarse nesting into an integer-indexed grid of aisles compatible with the shift and the autoequivalence.
- **claim-block-filtrations** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-block-filtrations)): The blocks are extension closed, and $\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}(\mathcal S_j,\mathcal S_k)=0$ whenever $j>k$. Every object has a finite triangle filtration with nonzero factors in blocks of strictly decreasing indices. In $\mathcal H_k$ there is a torsion pair $$\label{eq:block-torsion-pair} \begin{aligned} \mathcal T_k=V_{k+1}\cap\mathcal H_k &=\langle\mathcal S_{k+4},\mathcal S_{k+3}, \mathcal S_{k+2},\mathcal S_{k+1}\rangle,\\ \mathcal F_k&=\mathcal S_k. \end{aligned}$$ In particular, $\mathcal S_k$ is closed under subobjects in $\mathcal H_k$.
  - Explanation: Blocks are extension closed with one-directional Hom vanishing, every object has a finite filtration by blocks of decreasing index, and each heart carries a torsion pair whose torsion-free class is a single block.
- **claim-block-support** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-block-support)): For the fixed Euclidean norm on $K_{\mathrm{num}}(X)_\mathbb R$, there is a constant $C>0$ such that, for every $k\in\mathbb Z$ and every nonzero $E\in\mathcal S_k$, $$\label{eq:block-support} \begin{gathered} Z(E)\in\{t e^{i\pi\varphi}:t>0,\ k/5\le\varphi\le(k+1)/5\},\\ \left\lVert[E]\right\rVert\le C\|Z(E)\|. \end{gathered}$$
  - Explanation: Nonzero block objects have charge in a closed sector of controlled angle and satisfy the support inequality with a constant independent of the block index.
- **claim-block-hn** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-block-hn)): Every nonzero object of $\mathcal S_k$ has a finite filtration by short exact sequences in $\mathcal H_k$, with all intermediate quotients in $\mathcal S_k$, whose nonzero factors are block-semistable with strictly decreasing phases.
  - Explanation: Each nonzero block object has a finite refinement into block-semistable factors of strictly decreasing phase, using only discreteness of the full numerical lattice.
- **claim-block-hom-vanishing** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-block-hom)): If $E\in\mathcal S_k$ is block-semistable, then every nonzero subobject $G\subset E$ in $\mathcal H_k$ satisfies $\varphi_k(G)\le\varphi_k(E)$, without a saturation assumption. Consequently, for block-semistable $E,F\in\mathcal S_k$, $$\varphi_k(E)>\varphi_k(F)\quad\Longrightarrow\quad\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}(E,F)=0.$$
  - Explanation: Semistability in a block holds against all subobjects, not only saturated ones, and this forces Hom vanishing from larger to smaller phase.
- **claim-slicing** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-slicing)): The categories $\mathcal P(\varphi)$ form a slicing of $\mathcal D$ with finite Harder–Narasimhan filtrations. They satisfy $$\label{eq:slicing-covariance} \mathcal P(\varphi+1)=\mathcal P(\varphi)[1],\qquad \Phi\mathcal P(\varphi)=\mathcal P(\varphi+2/5) \quad(\varphi\in\mathbb R),$$ and every nonzero $E\in\mathcal P(\varphi)$ satisfies $$\label{eq:semistable-support} Z(E)\in\mathbb R_{>0}e^{i\pi\varphi},\qquad \left\lVert[E]\right\rVert\le C\|Z(E)\|.$$
  - Explanation: Extension closures of block-semistable objects form a slicing with finite Harder–Narasimhan filtrations, compatible with the shift and the autoequivalence, with positive charge on the phase ray and the support inequality.
- **claim-phase-cuts** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-phase-cuts)): For every real $c$, both pairs $$\label{eq:phase-cuts} \bigl(\mathcal P((c,\infty)),\mathcal P((-\infty,c])\bigr), \qquad \bigl(\mathcal P([c,\infty)),\mathcal P((-\infty,c))\bigr)$$ are an aisle and its right orthogonal for a bounded t-structure. Their hearts are respectively $\mathcal P((c,c+1])$ and $\mathcal P([c,c+1))$. Every interval category is the intersection of its lower and upper cut categories, with the indicated endpoint conventions. In particular, these categories, including each $\mathcal P(\varphi)$, are closed under direct summands.
  - Explanation: Cutting the slicing at any phase gives a bounded t-structure with explicit hearts, and every interval category, including each phase category, is closed under direct summands.
- **claim-interval-quasiabelian** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-interval-quasiabelian)): For every interval $I$ of width less than one, with any choices of open or closed endpoints, $\mathcal P(I)$ is quasi-abelian. Its strict exact sequences are exactly the short exact sequences of a suitable bounded heart whose three terms belong to $\mathcal P(I)$.
  - Explanation: A phase interval of width below one is quasi-abelian, with strict exact sequences being the short exact sequences of an ambient heart.
- **claim-local-finiteness** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-local-finiteness)): Every phase interval of width less than one generates a finite-length quasi-abelian category. Hence the slicing in Proposition <a href="#prop:slicing" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:slicing">16</a> is locally finite.
  - Explanation: Short phase intervals give finite-length quasi-abelian categories, so the slicing is locally finite, by a charge-projection argument that needs lattice discreteness but no discreteness of the charge image.

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-numerical",
        "exact_quote": "The map $v$ identifies $K_{\\mathrm{num}}(X)_{\\mathbb R}$ with $\\mathbb R^4$. Under this identification, $K_{\\mathrm{num}}(X)$ is a full discrete lattice contained in $$\\label{eq:numerical-lattice}\n \\mathbb Z\\times\\mathbb Z\\times\\tfrac1{10}\\mathbb Z\\times\\tfrac1{30}\\mathbb Z.$$ For $v=(r,c,d,e)$ and $w=(r',c',d',e')$, the Euler form is $$\\label{eq:euler-form}\n \\chi(v,w)=5\\left(re'-cd'+dc'-er'\n                   +\\frac56(rc'-cr')\\right).$$ The matrix induced by $\\Phi$ is $$\\label{eq:numerical-rotation}\n M=\n \\begin{pmatrix}\n -4&-20/3&-5&-5\\\\\n 1&1&0&0\\\\\n 1/2&1&1&0\\\\\n 1/6&1/2&1&1\n \\end{pmatrix},\n \\qquad\n \\det(zI-M)=z^4+z^3+z^2+z+1.$$",
        "source_id": "PM-055.1"
      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-central-charge",
        "exact_quote": "There is a unique numerical homomorphism $Z\\colon K_{\\mathrm{num}}(X)\\to\\mathbb C$ such that $Z\\circ\\Phi=\\lambda Z$ and $Z(\\mathcal O_x)=-1$ for closed points $x\\in X$. It is $$\\label{eq:central-charge}\n \\frac{Z(r,c,d,e)}5\n  =\\frac{\\lambda-1}{5}r\n   +\\left(t_0^2-\\frac56+\\frac{i t_0}{2}\\right)c\n   +\\left(-\\frac12+i t_0\\right)d-e.$$ In particular, $$\\label{eq:imaginary-charge}\n \\frac{\\operatorname{Im}Z(r,c,d,e)}{5t_0}=d+\\frac12c+ur.$$ For any fixed norm on $K_{\\mathrm{num}}(X)_{\\mathbb R}$, there is a constant $C_0>0$ such that every real numerical class $v=(r,c,d,e)$ satisfies $$\\label{eq:numerical-norm-control}\n \\left\\lVert v\\right\\rVert\\le C_0\\bigl(|r|+|c|+|Z(v)|\\bigr).$$",
        "source_id": "PM-055.1"
      },

        "exact_quote": "There is a fixed open square $$\\label{eq:fixed-omega}\n \\Omega=\\{\\eta=(\\eta_0,\\eta_1):|\\eta_0|,|\\eta_1|<5\\cdot10^{-6}\\}$$ and bounded aisles $U_\\eta=\\mathcal U_-^\\eta$, with hearts $\\mathcal A_\\eta=U_\\eta\\cap(U_\\eta[1])^\\perp$, such that $$\\label{eq:uniform-aisle}\n                    \\Phi U_\\eta\\subset U_\\theta\n                    \\qquad(\\eta,\\theta\\in\\Omega).$$ Moreover $$\\label{eq:perturbed-positive-form}\n N_\\eta=d+\\frac12c+ur+\\eta_1c+\\eta_0r\\geq0\n                    \\quad\\text{on }\\mathcal A_\\eta,\n \\qquad N_0=\\frac{\\operatorname{Im}Z}{5t_0},$$ and no nonzero object of any $\\mathcal A_\\eta$ has zero numerical class.       },
      "evidence": [

          "anchor": "stmt-prop-uniform-aisle",
          "exact_quote": "There is a fixed open square $$\\label{eq:fixed-omega}\n \\Omega=\\{\\eta=(\\eta_0,\\eta_1):|\\eta_0|,|\\eta_1|<5\\cdot10^{-6}\\}$$ and bounded aisles $U_\\eta=\\mathcal U_-^\\eta$, with hearts $\\mathcal A_\\eta=U_\\eta\\cap(U_\\eta[1])^\\perp$, such that $$\\label{eq:uniform-aisle}\n                    \\Phi U_\\eta\\subset U_\\theta\n                    \\qquad(\\eta,\\theta\\in\\Omega).$$ Moreover $$\\label{eq:perturbed-positive-form}\n N_\\eta=d+\\frac12c+ur+\\eta_1c+\\eta_0r\\geq0\n                    \\quad\\text{on }\\mathcal A_\\eta,\n \\qquad N_0=\\frac{\\operatorname{Im}Z}{5t_0},$$ and no nonzero object of any $\\mathcal A_\\eta$ has zero numerical class. The two parameters in <a href=\"#eq:uniform-aisle\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:uniform-aisle\">[eq:uniform-aisle]</a> vary independently throughout this same square.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-fine-grid",
        "exact_quote": "There is an open square $\\Omega_1$ about zero, contained in $\\Omega$, such that $$\\label{eq:single-shift-inclusion}\n D_3^\\alpha\\subset D_0^\\beta[1]\n \\qquad(\\alpha,\\beta\\in\\Omega_1).$$ For $k\\in\\mathbb Z$ and $\\eta\\in\\Omega_1$, define $$\\label{eq:fifths-aisles}\n V_k^\\eta=\\Phi^mU_\\eta[j],\\qquad k=2m+5j.$$ This is independent of the integers $m,j$ chosen, and these bounded aisles satisfy $$\\label{eq:fifths-nesting}\n \\begin{gathered}\n V_{k+1}^\\alpha\\subset V_k^\\beta,\\qquad\n V_{k+5}^\\eta=V_k^\\eta[1],\\qquad\n \\Phi V_k^\\eta=V_{k+2}^\\eta,\\\\\n k\\in\\mathbb Z,\\quad \\alpha,\\beta,\\eta\\in\\Omega_1.\n \\end{gathered}$$",
        "source_id": "PM-055.1"
      },

          "source_id": "PM-055.1"
        }

<a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **Gepner autoequivalence Phi** ([source](#stmt-lem-periodicity)): Let $\Phi=T_{\mathcal O_X}\circ(-\otimes\mathcal O_X(1))$.
- **Numerical Grothendieck group** ([source](#pm-h-2)): We write $K_{\mathrm{num}}(X)$ for the Grothendieck group modulo the radical of its Euler pairing, and $K_{\mathrm{num}}(X)_{\mathbb R}=K_{\mathrm{num}}(X)\otimes_{\mathbb Z}\mathbb R$.
- **Numerical central charge** ([source](#pm-h-2)): A numerical central charge is a homomorphism $Z\colon K_{\mathrm{num}}(X)\to\mathbb C$.
- **Numerical Bridgeland stability condition** ([source](#pm-h-2)): The pair $(Z,\mathcal P)$ is a numerical Bridgeland stability condition when these charge and slicing requirements hold, with local finiteness.
- **Normalized slope** ([source](#sec:numerical)): In particular, ordinary slope is normalized as $\mu_H(E)=c(E)/r(E)$ for a sheaf of positive rank.
- **Slope of a torsion-free sheaf** ([source](#sec:aisles)): Slope always means $\mu(F)=c(F)/r(F)$ for a torsion-free sheaf of positive rank.
- **Aisle and its heart** ([source](#sec:aisles)): $$ For $f$, this reflection exchanges the first and third affine pieces and fixes the second. This proves the comparison on $[0,1]$, and the theorem uses $\|\mu\|$ on the negative strip. For completeness, the symmetry is also compatible with the sheaf operations used here. Twisting by $\mathcal O_X(n)$ has the displayed effect on $(x,y)$ and preserves slope semistability. If $F$ is not reflexive, passing to $F^{**}$ preserves slope semistability and $r,c$, and only increases $d$. For a reflexive sheaf on a smooth threefold the higher sheaf Ext terms of its derived dual are supported in dimension zero, so its ordinary dual has characters $(r,-c,d)$ through degree two and is slope-semistable. The operations of dualizing and twisting by $\mathcal O_X(1)$ therefore give $(x,y)\mapsto(1-x,y+1/2-x)$ without changing the validity of an upper bound. Finally the three defining lines are nonpositive on $[0,1/2]$, and their maximum at $1/2$ is $-3/100$. ◻ </details> We use aisles in the convention $\mathcal D^{\leq0}$: they are closed under $[1]$, and the heart of an aisle $V$ is $V\cap(V[1])^\perp$, where $\perp$ denotes right Hom orthogonality.
- **Right orthogonal** ([source](#pm-h-5)): For a subcategory $\mathcal C$, the notation $\mathcal C^\perp$ means its right $\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}$-orthogonal.
- **Extension closure** ([source](#pm-h-5)): Extension closures always include the zero object and finite iterated extensions.
- **High and low objects** ([source](#sec:aisles)): Call a nonzero object of $\mathcal B_b$ *high* if $p_b=0$ or $N_b^\eta>0$, and *low* otherwise.
- **Block** ([source](#pm-h-4)): The category $\mathcal S_k=V_k\cap V_{k+1}^\perp$ between consecutive aisles is called a block; here $\perp$ is right $\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}$-orthogonality.
- **Phase of a block object** ([source](#pm-h-11)): Each nonzero $E\in\mathcal S_k$ has a unique phase $\varphi_k(E)\in[a,b]$ with $Z(E)=\|Z(E)\|e^{i\pi\varphi_k(E)}$.
- **Saturated subobject in the block** ([source](#pm-h-11)): A subobject $F\subset E$ in $\mathcal H_k$ is called *saturated in the block* if $E/F\in\mathcal S_k$.
- **Block-semistable object** ([source](#pm-h-11)): We call $E$ *block-semistable* if $$\varphi_k(F)\le\varphi_k(E) \quad\text{for every nonzero subobject saturated in the block.}$$
- **Phase category P(phi)** ([source](#pm-h-12)): For $\varphi\in\mathbb R$, let $\mathcal P(\varphi)$ be the extension closure of all block-semistable objects whose assigned phase equals $\varphi$.

      "exact_definition": "$$ For $f$, this reflection exchanges the first and third affine pieces and fixes the second. This proves the comparison on $[0,1]$, and the theorem uses $|\\mu|$ on the negative strip. For completeness, the symmetry is also compatible with the sheaf operations used here. Twisting by $\\mathcal O_X(n)$ has the displayed effect on $(x,y)$ and preserves slope semistability. If $F$ is not reflexive, passing to $F^{**}$ preserves slope semistability and $r,c$, and only increases $d$. For a reflexive sheaf on a smooth threefold the higher sheaf Ext terms of its derived dual are supported in dimension zero, so its ordinary dual has characters $(r,-c,d)$ through degree two and is slope-semistable. The operations of dualizing and twisting by $\\mathcal O_X(1)$ therefore give $(x,y)\\mapsto(1-x,y+1/2-x)$ without changing the validity of an upper bound. Finally the three defining lines are nonpositive on $[0,1/2]$, and their maximum at $1/2$ is $-3/100$. ◻ </details> We use aisles in the convention $\\mathcal D^{\\leq0}$: they are closed under $[1]$, and the heart of an aisle $V$ is $V\\cap(V[1])^\\perp$, where $\\perp$ denotes right Hom orthogonality.",

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **step-periodicity** [claim](#stmt-lem-periodicity) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-local-finiteness-2): Relative Fourier–Mukai kernels and successive mutations against the ambient projective space, then Koszul vanishing and a derived Nakayama argument. -> Natural isomorphism of the fifth iterate with the double shift.
- **step-numerical-lattice** [claim](#stmt-lem-numerical) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-numerical): Weak Lefschetz and Riemann–Roch to compute the Chern character, Euler form and the matrix of the autoequivalence. -> Identification of the real numerical group with four-dimensional space and a discrete lattice.
- **step-central-charge** [claim](#stmt-prop-central-charge) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-central-charge): Solve the eigenvalue equation column by column with the point normalization; invertibility of a coordinate map gives norm control. -> Unique normalized central charge with explicit formulas and norm control.
- **step-slope-input** [claim](#stmt-lem-slope-input) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-slope-input): Compare the cited stronger inequality with a three-piece envelope using reflection symmetry. -> Envelope bound for semistable sheaves.
- **step-first-tilt-noetherian** [claim](#stmt-lem-first-tilt-noetherian) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-first-tilt-noetherian): Stabilize epimorphism chains through a discrete nonnegative invariant, ranks, and increasing chains of subsheaves of a reflexive sheaf. -> Noetherian first tilted hearts.
- **step-second-tilt** [claim](#stmt-lem-second-tilt) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-second-tilt): High objects are closed under extensions; maximal torsion subobjects exist by noetherianity. -> Torsion pair and double-tilt heart with nonnegative form.
- **step-compare-cuts** [claim](#stmt-lem-compare-slope-cuts) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-compare-slope-cuts): Reduce to Hom vanishing between torsion and torsion-free classes, then reach a sign contradiction using rank bounds from the slope envelope. -> Inclusion of the positive aisle in the negative aisle.
- **step-twist-aisle** [claim](#stmt-lem-twist-aisle) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-twist-aisle): Place the structure sheaf in the hearts, then use Serre duality and extension closure on the twist triangle. -> Spherical twist maps the positive aisle into the negative aisle.
- **step-uniform-aisle** [claim](#stmt-prop-uniform-aisle) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-uniform-aisle): Hyperplane twist shears the parameters; combine with the twist inclusion. -> Uniform independent-parameter inclusion on a fixed square.
- **step-fine-grid** [claim](#stmt-prop-fine-grid) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-fine-grid): Compactness argument with limits of normalized classes and four half-plane inequalities, then truncation. -> Nested grid of aisles compatible with shift and autoequivalence.
- **step-block-filtrations** [claim](#stmt-lem-block-filtrations) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-block-filtrations): Iterated truncation along the grid and orthogonality. -> Block filtrations and a torsion pair in each heart.
- **step-block-support** [claim](#stmt-prop-block-support) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-block-support): Two adjacent hearts locate the charge sector; perturbed positivity with independent signs controls rank and first Chern coordinate. -> Block charge sector and uniform support inequality.
- **step-block-hn** [claim](#stmt-lem-block-hn) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-block-hn): Maximal phase and magnitude among saturated subobjects, see-saw property, and a length bound from a positive additive projection. -> Finite refinement into block-semistable factors.
- **step-block-hom** [claim](#stmt-lem-block-hom) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-block-hom): Saturate a destabilizing subobject via the block torsion pair and use sector-width arguments. -> Unsaturated test and Hom vanishing.
- **step-slicing** [claim](#stmt-prop-slicing) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-slicing): Hom vanishing by extension induction, splice block filtrations by the octahedral axiom, merge equal phases, transport by shift and autoequivalence. -> Slicing with finite Harder–Narasimhan filtrations and covariance.
- **step-phase-cuts** [claim](#stmt-lem-phase-cuts) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-phase-cuts): Truncation triangle from cutting the filtration, orthogonal characterizations, and an interval analysis. -> Bounded t-structures at every phase and summand closure.
- **step-interval-quasiabelian** [claim](#stmt-lem-interval-quasiabelian) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-interval-quasiabelian): Realize the interval as a torsion-free class in a phase-cut heart and verify quasi-abelian axioms through ambient universal properties. -> Quasi-abelian structure on short intervals.
- **step-local-finiteness** [claim](#stmt-prop-local-finiteness) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-local-finiteness): Additive positive projection of the charge bounds the length of strict filtrations. -> Finite length of short interval categories.
- **step-main-theorem** [claim](#stmt-thm-main) / [proof](#stmt-prop-local-finiteness): Assemble charge, slicing, local finiteness, support property and covariance. -> Gepner-covariant numerical stability condition on every smooth quintic threefold.

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **inv-numerical-rotation-charpoly**: In the normalized coordinates on the numerical group, the action of the autoequivalence is a rank-four matrix whose characteristic polynomial is z^4+z^3+z^2+z+1, so its eigenvalues are the nonidentity fifth roots of unity.
- **inv-tensor-then-twist-coordinates**: The action on coordinates factors as tensoring by the hyperplane bundle followed by the spherical twist, which changes only the rank coordinate, by subtracting the Euler characteristic of the tensored class.
- **inv-fifth-power-identity-on-lattice**: Because the characteristic polynomial divides z^5-1, the fifth power of the coordinate matrix is the identity, matching the periodicity relation of the functor with an even shift acting trivially on numerical classes.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **anti-finite-check-as-existence-proof**: Treat the exact matrix computation as evidence that a stability condition with the stated covariance exists on the derived category. -> The matrix only records the numerical action on a four-dimensional space; slicing, Harder-Narasimhan filtrations, support and local finiteness are statements about the infinite derived category and are not touched by it.
- **anti-other-threefold-matrix**: Reuse the displayed matrix and its characteristic polynomial for a threefold other than a smooth quintic. -> The entries come from the specific Euler form and Todd class of the quintic; another threefold has a different lattice and a different action, so the polynomial and the periodicity need not hold.
- **anti-floating-point-lattice**: Implement the lattice coordinates and the matrix action in floating point and read off discreteness or exact periodicity. -> Rounding hides the fractional denominators of the lattice and can make an exact identity such as the fifth power being the identity look approximately true or false.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `algorithmic`. The paper's main existence theorem is not finite, but its numerical backbone is a finite exact computation: the action of the autoequivalence on normalized coordinates, obtained as a hyperplane twist followed by the rank correction, equals the displayed matrix, and its fifth power is the identity. This is checked in exact rational arithmetic on sample classes; it does not certify the categorical statements.

```python
# INPUTS: one line with four rationals r c d e (normalized character vector), written as integers or p/q.
# PRECONDITIONS: exactly four tokens, each parseable as an exact rational.
# OUTPUTS: line 1 = image of the vector under tensoring by the hyperplane bundle then the spherical twist; line 2 = fifth iterate of that map.
# TIME: constant number of rational operations per application of the map.
# SPACE: constant.
# ARITHMETIC: exact rational arithmetic with Fraction; no floating point.
# FAILURE: malformed input raises an exception and exits nonzero.
import sys
from fractions import Fraction

def phi(v):
    r, c, d, e = v
    c1 = c + r
    d1 = d + c + r / 2
    e1 = e + d + c / 2 + r / 6
    chi = 5 * (e1 + Fraction(5, 6) * c1)
    r2 = r - chi
    return (r2, c1, d1, e1)

v = tuple(Fraction(x) for x in sys.stdin.read().split())
assert len(v) == 4
x = v
for _ in range(5):
    x = phi(x)
print(" ".join(str(t) for t in phi(v)))
print(" ".join(str(t) for t in x))
```

<a id="pm-s5"></a>
## 5. Domain engineering contracts

| domain | applies | strength | maturity | basis | why |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| software | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The result is an existence theorem in derived algebraic geometry. It gives no executable procedure or interface that a software library could implement. |
| games_simulation | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The numerical group is a four-dimensional real space attached to a complex threefold. It carries no dynamics, state evolution or physical simulation content. |
| tools | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The central charge has a closed coordinate formula, but it only evaluates on numerical classes. A tool would also need the full category of coherent sheaves and its semistable objects, and the paper gives no way to compute those. |
| algorithms | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Finite length of phase intervals is shown by a counting argument, and Harder–Narasimhan filtrations are produced by selecting maximal phases. Neither gives a terminating procedure on sheaves, since subobjects are not enumerated and no computational model is given. |
| data_structures | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Block filtrations are categorical decompositions of objects of a triangulated category. They are not a representable data layout with operations and costs. |
| hardware_accelerators | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The periodicity relation for the autoequivalence is a functor isomorphism proved with kernels. It has no arithmetic kernel or dataflow that could be mapped onto hardware. |
| crypto_security | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The statements concern torsion pairs in tilted hearts. No hardness assumption, adversary model or security property appears anywhere in the paper. |
| machine_learning | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The support estimate bounds numerical classes by the modulus of the central charge, with a constant coming from finite-dimensional linear algebra. It involves no data, training or generalization. |
| optimisation_operations_research | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Maximal-phase selection in block refinement picks extremes among finitely many numerical values to prove existence. It is not an objective-driven optimization with a solver or feasibility structure. |
| distributed_systems | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Quasi-abelian exact structures on phase intervals describe categories of sheaf complexes. They say nothing about processes, messages, consensus or fault tolerance. |
| other | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The natural audience is algebraic geometry and mathematical physics, as a rigorous existence result for Gepner-type stability. It could also serve as a future formal-verification target, but no encoding is supplied here. |

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **limit-smooth-quintic-only** (hypothesis): The existence theorem is proved only for a smooth complex quintic threefold in four-dimensional projective space, with the hyperplane twist followed by the spherical twist at the structure sheaf. The numerical group, the eigencharge and the periodicity relation all rely on this specific geometry. Blocked use: ['Applying the stability condition to singular quintics or to other Calabi–Yau threefolds', 'Assuming the same phase increment for an arbitrary autoequivalence or an untwisted line-bundle twist']
- **limit-slope-envelope-external-input** (hypothesis): The bound on the normalized second character of slope-semistable sheaves is a consequence of an external stronger Bogomolov–Gieseker inequality. It holds only for torsion-free slope-semistable sheaves of bounded slope, and the margins of the second tilt depend on it. Blocked use: ['Using the envelope for sheaves that are not torsion-free or not slope-semistable', 'Using it for slopes outside the stated range without the twist and reflection reductions']
- **limit-second-tilt-parameter-range** (edge_case): The torsion pair of the second cut is proved only for the two first-tilt slopes and perturbation parameters in the small fixed square. The positivity margins are checked for these specific constants, so larger perturbations or other first-tilt slopes are not covered. Blocked use: ['Enlarging the perturbation square without rechecking the margins', 'Using a first-tilt slope other than the two stated values']
- **limit-block-refinement-existential** (nonconstructive): The refinement of a block object into semistable factors selects a saturated subobject of maximal phase over finitely many numerical values. It gives existence of the filtration, not a procedure that enumerates or computes subobjects. Blocked use: ['Presenting the refinement as a terminating algorithm on sheaves', 'Deriving a running-time bound for computing Harder–Narasimhan filtrations']
- **limit-lattice-discreteness-not-charge-image** (formal_scope): Local finiteness and the block refinement use discreteness of the full numerical lattice. They do not give discreteness of the charge image, which need not hold. Blocked use: ['Assuming the set of charges or phases of objects is discrete', 'Replacing the lattice argument by one on the charge image alone']
- **limit-interval-width-below-one** (hypothesis): The quasi-abelian structure and finite length are established only for phase intervals of width less than one. <a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


We prove Toda's normalized quintic Gepner conjecture. On every smooth complex quintic threefold, we construct a numerical Bridgeland stability condition for which tensoring by the hyperplane bundle, followed by the spherical twist at the structure sheaf, increases phase by 2/5.
